# Sylvia GM Alpha Completion Plan

> **Status:** Active planning and cross-environment handoff authority
>
> **Endpoint:** A Windows Alpha candidate accepted by the project owner; publication requires separate approval.
>
> **Updated:** 2026-10-17

PES-06 scope correction, 2026-09-15: the project owner permits starting a new campaign for current panel
verification. Existing-campaign rules updates, including preserving layout when new panels are
added, moved from PES-119 to PL-01 on 2026-09-16 at the project owner's direction. PL-01 is retained
local post-launch planning evidence, not a file distributed in this repository. This is post-launch work, not a PES or Alpha completion blocker.

This is the repository status document synchronized with Project Desk (the project owner's current planning and
decision source). Completed detail belongs in Git history and focused tests. Historical plans,
handoffs and checkout-specific records do not activate work.

## 1. Current work

**17 October 2026: reviewed combined source and final-sprint repairs.** The combined source is
maintained at [pneuma-io/sylvia-gm](https://github.com/pneuma-io/sylvia-gm). The repository remains
private. The reviewed code/content import is commit `cebc66f2245af071f6b2f4cfdeeefbeb980a70de`,
followed by the README correction `56b0164885834ccac2a1c6f1f98607da2b0202a1`. Subsequent scoped
repairs are recorded in Git history and the delivery receipt; those references do not certify an
older executable for newer source bytes.

The selected implementation scope covers the collated repository findings: public-facing documentation,
portable packaging, focused CI coverage, generic procedure failure handling, bounded modifier
evaluation, the shipping build profile, dataset-safe launch behavior and invocation-owned build
cleanup. The integration worker remains the sole source writer. Disk classification remains a
separate read-only task. No public release or existing-data deletion is selected by this sprint.

| Area | Current verified basis and remaining acceptance |
| --- | --- |
| Launch content | SRD Lite 0.3.12: 117 supported spells, without consumables. Its primary content identity is `d93c0d4f6eff9b24e475f4f9b178ce11cfcad889cb3dc331db87562d78c92efe`. Future SRD Full remains separately preserved and inactive. |
| Combined source | The private root import and all 2,716 blob paths/hashes/modes were independently checked against the reviewed source. The naming correction passed 41 affected source checks, all 17 Plan admissions, fresh package reproduction and old-campaign pin persistence. These results do not establish a fresh installed candidate. |
| Current repairs and CI | Procedure failures and nested calls use the admitted tool/trigger contract; the modifier helper uses bounded evaluation. Normal push/PR checks include existing protected-settings, portable identity, release exclusions, bundled notices, product identity, release assembly and runner-selection tests alongside supported Python/JS journeys. Windows Cargo tests are separate. Real-model and installed/VM checks remain separate. |
| Packaging | The tracked release recipe assembles an unsigned NSIS installer and portable EXE ZIP from exact source/artifact inventories, with required notices and no mutable user data. Shipping uses the normal optimized release profile. Final rebuilt binaries and both distribution formats still need exact-byte qualification after the preservation/space sequence. Older native evidence cannot certify the changed identity, content or runtime. |
| Data and credentials | Installed and portable launches select their own data. The existing per-database owner lock prevents concurrent writers to the same dataset. Verify final packaged coexistence, same-dataset refusal, folder move/update and save/reopen in Windows. DPAPI credentials moved to another Windows account require re-entry; do not copy credentials into evidence. |
| Notices and rights | Preserve the standard PolyForm base and adopted Additional Permissions revision 3 (16 October 2026), their scope, exclusions and prior grants. The historical 108 rows are technical/access qualifications to reconcile, not 108 owner decisions. Specific uncovered-rights and provenance questions remain separate. |
| Presentation and status | README and About use the personally reviewed public-facing copy. The authority index, product/architecture contracts and development process are present. Historical/local scratch evidence stays excluded; it is not a required public setup dependency. |

The remaining sequence is: finish and review the scoped repairs and reproducible packaging;
preserve unique ongoing work in reviewed Git/PR form and complete the separately approved disk-space
work; then rebuild and test the portable ZIP and installer on clean Windows. Reconcile exact
candidate evidence, then pause for go-live planning. Public publication requires separate approval.
The dedicated final native-input check remains separate; this source sprint does not close it.

The Licensor adopted Additional Permissions revision 3 on 16 October 2026 for material he is
authorised to license, with its stated exclusions. See [licensing status](../../LICENSING-STATUS.md).
This records no official release, legal clearance for unrecorded rights, or installer acceptance.

**Historical integration evidence (15–16 October):** Build1970/Lite0.3.9 and cleanup source
`466c5eb55d58ac5f783cb01f10bda1b9505165b33402f4b38023b27a60662d75` are retained inputs,
not the current combined candidate. Their reports recorded 164 supply checks/two genuine Sol
exchanges and 104 cleanup checks/17 Plan admissions respectively, with their original fixture,
provider and native qualifications. Reuse unchanged evidence; do not turn it into a new full
campaign, spell-roster replay or final installer result.

Development qualification has no whole-run wall-time cutoff. Normal pushes and PRs use this mode;
the explicitly selected future post-launch CI mode uses `--ci` for its 1,800-second test budget.
Required missing, failed or skipped checks cannot produce a green complete gate. Provider,
installed/VM and final native-input acceptance are not implied by a source CI result.

**SRD spell integration and coordinate combat, 2026-10-11:** the project owner authorizes completing the approved
96 SRD Lite spells against full coordinate combat with a map. Precise positioning matters in combat;
exploration and ordinary roleplay do not require map movement. Combat movement, range, targeting and area
effects must use the same authoritative positions, reusing existing spatial and map groundwork.
Existing SRD rules and approved spell adaptations govern coordinate positioning. Trigger Discipline retains its grid. The product and architecture contracts own these
requirements; the old SRD post-Alpha positioning/map deferral is superseded.

### Prior documentation selection (historical for this spell task)

**Re-centering, 2026-10-02:** Fluff coordinates bounded Codex tasks from existing Project Desk
decisions, with file ownership, acceptance criteria and a mandatory return report. The current
selection is context/documentation cleanup only. Gameplay implementation and spell integration
are paused until separately agreed; previous root/worker assignments no longer authorize work.
The paused spell handover released integration ownership and preserves independent dirty worktrees.

Public Alpha includes D&D SRD 5.2.1, Severed Lands and No Ruleset. TD/Siren are later and not Alpha
blockers. Full fixed-rule SRD spells remain pre-release scope, with open fictional adjudication
left to the GM. The cancelled AR-01 marathon is replaced by comprehensive automated testing plus
short targeted real-model and installed checks; continuity, progression, recovery and user journeys
remain required evidence. The 600-second aggregate gate stays; package generation has no such
timeout. Final coverage and acceptance remain unverified by this cleanup.

the project owner's selected licensing direction is PolyForm Noncommercial 1.0.0 plus Sylvia Additional
Permissions. LRD-09 owns final wording, rights review and adoption. This describes the selected
direction only; adoption is incomplete and existing licensing grants remain unchanged.

Existing Codex credits are authorized for the bounded task; purchases/top-ups, publication, pushes,
branch/code integration, package regeneration and live campaigns are outside this selection.

**Operating the guest:** use [`scripts/windows_test_vm.ps1`](../../scripts/windows_test_vm.ps1)
with `-Config D:/VMs/Sylvia-Test/runner.json`. `Status` checks its identity; `Start` uses a headless
frontend; `Shutdown` requests an orderly guest shutdown. `Run -Code '<PowerShell>'` executes in the
logged-in guest session. Launch the installed app with this guest code:

```powershell
& powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File C:\SylviaTest\runner\start-sylvia-test.ps1
```

That helper selects `C:/SylviaTest/profiles/qualification/authority.sqlite3`, a separate WebView
profile and Full Audit. For fixture checks only, the adjacent `start-qualification-fixture.ps1`
starts the scripted service on guest loopback port 18746. `Run` can execute
`C:\SylviaTest\runner\node.exe C:\SylviaTest\runner\inspect_installed_webview.cjs` to read the actual
app; the source is [`scripts/inspect_installed_webview.cjs`](../../scripts/inspect_installed_webview.cjs).
New test scripts belong in the guest runner folder and use its existing `playwright-core`,
`connectOverCDP` with `noDefaults: true`, and real controls. Keep the selected profile and provider
explicit when starting a different journey.

`CopyTo` takes one local file and a guest directory; `CopyFrom` takes a guest file and a new local
filename. Both reject existing destinations and verify SHA-256. VirtualBox 7.2.14's `--no-replace`
produced empty files in the pilot, so the helper performs its own existence check and verification.
`Screenshot -Destination <new.png>` captures only the guest. `Click -X <x> -Y <y>` and
`Drag -X <x> -Y <y> -ToX <x> -ToY <y>` use fresh guest screenshot coordinates, starting at zero;
`Keys -Scancodes <hex bytes>` sends guest keyboard input with explicit key releases. These native
actions use VirtualBox's console, not host input. Close Audit as well as the main window when
verifying complete app shutdown; its separate window keeps the process alive. Preserve needed Audit
before closing, and power down the guest when idle to release its RAM.

1. **Behavioural** covers launch bugs, selected app improvements and the supporting response
   quality investigation. It includes AR-20, PES-129/130/132/133/134, PL-06 and PES-01, the older v137 Ready-summary
   report, LRD-06, and MQ-01/02/03. LRD-06 has a selected scope for

   its remaining questions as one assessment using the completed MQ-05/06 and other existing
   evidence first, not separate full campaign runs per finding.
   Existing attribution requirements and the limit on mandatory model benchmarking still apply.
2. **AR-01 is the final Behavioural ticket.** Finish and verify preceding selected changes and
   resolve or explicitly disposition their findings before the fresh six-act run. AR-03/04/05's
   fixes are recorded as resolved; reconcile their evidence with the candidate selected for that
   run. Preserve the failed P62 campaign. AR-01 retains its existing blind-player, detour,
   compaction, restart and failure criteria; it does not replace focused acceptance for Builder,
   exports or other selected changed journeys, nor certify every possible app behavior.
3. **Final cleanup** follows behavioural readiness: LRD-01/02/03/04/05/07/08/09, AH-07 and the new
   [FC-01 public-repository review](../../.scratch/final-cleanup/issues/01-review-public-repository-contents.md).
   Check licensing/provenance, notices, product/publisher naming, credential protection, app and website claims,
   current documentation and what belongs in the public repository and release. LRD-09 coordinates
   rights/licence work; its narrower tickets own their findings. FC-01 owns repository privacy and
   publication hygiene without repeating legal-source investigations. Privacy boundaries apply to
   the review itself, including sealed ComfyUI content. This category is preparation, not publication.
4. **Post-launch** contains AH-01 through AH-06, PL-01 through PL-05, PL-07 through PL-09 and the TD-specific MQ-04
   comparison. The AH tasks improve maintainability or investigate future design without a
   demonstrated launch defect requiring them. MQ-04 follows Trigger Discipline's deferral; its
   original failure and attribution remain recorded. AH-04's memory investigation is also deferred.

If Final cleanup reveals a runtime, security or playable-content defect, return the correction to

what that run proved; cosmetic notices alone do not require repeating the full campaign. Parent
tickets reuse their children's evidence without silently closing distinct acceptance requirements.

returns with the saved campaign. The manual Settings probe again landed after loading, so input
responsiveness during a sustained installed load remains to be demonstrated. Source now includes

lookup that retains interrupted-session recovery. Focused paging, transport, frontend and Rust

2026-09-08: Ruleset > Setting > Plan inheritance, with contained snapshots, local customization and
explicit adoption of source updates. Rulesets must not declare Settings. The earlier independent
pairing proposal is superseded. the project owner approved editable TD cover heights (Low 0.6 m, Chest-high 1.2 m,
Full-wall 2.4 m) and selected the existing MiniMax M3 connection for PES-01 installed tests. The
recorded product and test-provider questions are answered; implementation is underway.
Generic Start now copies the Plan's authored mechanical state alongside the chosen party. Explicit
Plan placement slots bind the assigned participant IDs and names, apply authored character fields,
and append map tokens or roster entries in player-first selection order. Source checks retain world
actors and adjacent state through Start, retry and reopen without changing the source Plan or
Character Files. Missing slots or lists, duplicate identities and foreign/reserved state reject.
TD turn handoff now uses an authored roster and each actor's current AP limit. A composed source

The independent panel corrections and package preparation are verified below. Root owns the live

The inheritance correction now admits package format 4 (the portable archive structure) with a
Setting-owned Ruleset snapshot. New Builder Rulesets use that format; legacy packages remain
readable. The picker discovers Settings independently and uses their contained Ruleset identities.
Saved Plan selection and later Builder launch retain the Plan's original package bytes after source
updates. Setting publication now copies the saved Ruleset, admits authored world files, and makes
the complete Setting selectable. Local Ruleset file replacements affect that copy only. Focused
checks cover restart, export/import into an empty library, mechanical customization, rejected invalid
runtime, repeat publication, and retained Interview choices. An SRD startup read-limit error found
through those checks is corrected. The product and architecture contracts reflect the project owner's inheritance
correction. Plans now save their complete Setting package and export/import through the native
content commands as `.sylvia-gm-plan` files. Source checks cover fresh-library import, exact repeat
import/export, reopened sources, later Builder references, stale export rejection and malformed
archive rejection. Bundled SRD and Severed Lands now use format 4 with Setting-owned Ruleset copies.
The Rift party template travels inside the Setting; an exported/imported Rift Plan reaches ordinary
Start with that saved template even when the application default is unavailable. The imported Plan
keeps its Rift setup route. These checks use synthetic providers or no provider, not installed UI.
Independent Plan publication now applies local replacements, additions and removals to inherited
Setting and Ruleset files, derives the resulting bindings, and saves a portable copy after runtime
validation. The actual Builder tool loop passes repeat-save, invalid-runtime and export/import checks
without changing the source packages. The Interview asks optional questions after content selection,
from the exact selected copies. Source checks cover all three question owners, absent groups,
reselection, restart, answer replay, and retaining a Setting selection across a catalog refresh.
Independent Builders can now inspect other installed sources by exact candidate and package
identity, including a standalone Ruleset update for a Setting or Plan. The real Builder tool loop
checks source filtering, bounded reads, rejected paths, restart and unchanged launch references.
Builder can also edit owned package declarations and explicitly adopt complete installed sources
before applying its retained custom edits. Actual Builder publication checks cover new tools,
character-schema changes, added support dependencies and local world/rule customizations for both
Ruleset and Setting updates. The resulting tools execute and the complete Plan survives export/import;
sources stay unchanged. A new Setting likewise publishes updated declarations and support files.
An older exact Setting selection now launches Builder even after a standalone Ruleset update changes
the picker. Conflict-resolution conversation and creating the TD test world through the installed
UI remain for MiniMax M3 testing.
Focused bundled Plan, source-selection, Builder and
campaign-start checks pass, as do executable party-picker tests, touched Python lint, JavaScript

TD's compiler now emits format 4 with a standalone Ruleset and a conformance Setting that owns its

The TD control/package selection passes 23 checks and 12 subtests; the separate authored-campaign

`6016489bcfea5f2986626186319aa5356973a5ebc944b47df36d35a1d1c7ce48`.
No provenance check was bypassed. The Sylvia selection passes 26 checks across party placement/Start, Plan bundles,
edited Plan publication and complete source adoption. These remain source tests, not installed

without partial changes. Builder's TD authoring reference now travels with the Ruleset and survives

PES-02 now creates independent saved Builder chats with the original button, Interview context,
exact package identities and captured Plan. Starting Builder uses application context without
invented player speech. The Interview keeps its answers and revision. Builder reads its original
sources on demand even after Interview reselection; exact saved package identities are rechecked
before authoring. The former bundled picker ID remains stable, with aliases for identical installed
bytes. Shared workspace replacement/deletion requires the file version returned by a read or save;
outdated writes and competing creates reject without overwriting newer content.

The frontend keeps generation, draft acceptance, steering, Stop, retry, polling and heartbeat state
per session and attempt. Navigation and late replies cannot redirect another chat's output or
controls. Reconnect retains multiple streams and accepts the former single-stream hint. Builder
outcomes are saved as completed/stopped/interrupted evidence; an unfinished accepted input after
owner restart projects interrupted, preserves work, and requires explicit continuation. Internal

Independent character Builders now save against the original installed Ruleset schema and Setting,
including closed non-D&D schemas. Role remains in library metadata and enters participant data only
when the schema declares it; multiple schemas require an explicit choice. The catalog discovers

artifacts retain their setup behavior. Saved generic Plans can be selected as later Builder
references. Plan and character entries appear only after their complete files are ready; readers
preserve exact Markdown line endings when checking content hashes.

Root reviewed and integrated Sol's isolated participant-schema and generic Plan-library drafts.

Earlier focused evidence: six library/title checks pass, including real owner-transport saves for

The owner transport now supplies compatible party options for a saved generic Plan and accepts

Existing content-creation chats now migrate into independent saved chats on owner startup. Their

The participant screen now loads compatible generic library choices, preserves explicit selections
on return, and leaves newly saved characters unselected until the player chooses them. Late catalog

transport checks pass. The migration/library run passed 20 checks; its remaining name-only-reference

Unfinished Interviews now support rename, archive, restore, trash and permanent deletion before

Root integrated and revised Sol's Builder-chat deletion draft. A separate sidebar control requires
confirmation, rejects active work, and removes only that independent chat and its workspace. The

now exercises 28 JavaScript scenarios. Rust compilation and Python lint pass.

PES-03 now resolves a Ruleset's card declaration into saved display values when its action runs and
renders those values with a shared layout. Ten focused checks pass, including an action without dice,

pre-existing failure when exporting an attack-only value for a non-attack test, by initializing

card check. No PES-03 completion is claimed.

PES-04 now rejects direct map actions while GM work is active and prevents GM work from starting
during direct mechanics. Panel controls receive current availability from the owner. Direct actions
save their origin beside the actual result, including through a pending choice and restart. Exact
retries return the saved receipt; changed selections under the same identity reject. The GM receives
these saved outcomes as application data without a fabricated provider tool call. Six focused owner,
concurrency, provider-input and real package-choice checks pass. Twenty adjacent map/network checks
passed; the stale frontend source assertion was updated to the captured campaign ID and passes.
Ruleset metadata now selects immediate execution or exact preview confirmation, and automatic or
explicit GM response. Confirmation rejects changed selections and stale revisions. The response uses
a saved application event in the originating session, preserving typed IC/OOC drafts and unrelated
Builder activity. The real provider adapter receives the existing action result and a current response
task. A connection failure, owner restart and response retry retain one action and its exact result.
Repeated response requests cannot start another generation for that action. Required GM transitions
and Campaign File updates keep the existing loop. These are source checks, not installed acceptance.

Root integrated Sol's reusable-library deletion draft and tightened cleanup restoration. Characters,
Companions and Plans have separate delete controls with exact backend confirmation and content identity.
Deletion rejects linked paths, preserves campaigns/chats and removes only the chosen local entry.
If file cleanup fails, removed files are restored so the entry remains catalog-visible and retryable;
an unsuccessful restoration reports it unavailable. The four focused deletion checks cover both
participant and Plan cleanup. Executable frontend checks cover confirmation, late navigation,
automatic and explicit action replies, and retention of unsent drafts. The changed provider path also
passes initial control-response and response-retry checks through Campaign File completion. Adjacent
opening context and retry-history checks pass. One older participant test expected the former inline
refresh expression; it now checks the existing navigation-guarded refresh and passes.

The earlier combined card/action/session/package check passed 21 tests, including the frontend wrapper

The current action-response/library/session selection passes all 28 Python checks. Seven selected

Card buttons now resolve from the saved action and button index through the same preview and

The combined shared card/action/Party/scope/map selection passed 37 Python checks, including the

Seven focused availability checks pass, including no effects/dice during previews, spent action

The real path-bound owner action now receives the complete authoritative scene; the focused check
proves its route avoids a hidden occupying actor. This restores an argument dropped during the

Root integrated and corrected Sol's TD compiler draft on TD `09238868`. Allied/player and hostile/GM

Four focused TD integration checks and three firing-mode subtests pass against Sylvia's actual

TD now shares its existing AP, readiness, ammunition, cycling, drone and network prerequisite

Seven TD integration checks with six subtests pass through Sylvia's runtime, including weapon
cycling and spent AP, network actions, the discounted AP boundary and lobby foothold spending.
No package was generated or installed. Movement route cost and attack geometry still need PES-05.

PES-05 now distinguishes physical edge barriers from occupied grid cells. Existing geometry defaults
to occupied cells; `occupies_cells: false` retains the physical box without reserving adjacent cells.
Pathfinding sweeps the moving character's actual box or circular footprint through each step.
Focused checks cover adjacent walking lanes, sealed corners, exact-fit openings, circular clearance,
an open hinged door's remaining obstruction, retraction, and prior snapshot stability. The combined
selection of spatial, map, control and compiler checks passes (98 checks); unrelated SRD mechanics
were excluded. No installed geometry acceptance is claimed.

Root integrated Sol's map presentation draft after correcting occupied-cell shading to match the
engine. The existing map now has compact numeric heights, optional Ruleset height labels, keyboard
selection and exact inspection, and a Cover info toggle that adds labels without hiding geometry.
Inactive/retracted objects leave no shape or occupied-cell fill. Three focused renderer checks pass.
TD's current wall and security door opt into physical barriers; its existing cover object retains

The Builder compiler and TD descriptor now declare `spatial_clearance.v1` support. Metric cover
preset defaults remain unselected; action-specific preview integration is recorded below.
No new package or installer has been generated.

Player-control checks can now read the current spatial scene and calculate local scalar values
before evaluating prerequisites. This supports real route costs without running effects, spending

Root integrated Sol's TD movement draft: the existing route query and exact AP expression now each
have one helper shared by eligibility and execution. Nine TD integration checks with six subtests

The PES-05 backend now projects action-specific route, firing-line and silhouette previews from
the same declared spatial queries and calculations. Display queries use only the renderer's known
objects, while eligibility and commitment retain the complete scene. Three owner checks pass:
hidden obstacle changes leave the entire displayed preview unchanged but alter the committed route;
blocked/unaffordable selections retain honest estimates; and real muzzle-height queries produce
fractional exposure without effects or state writes. Thirty adjacent player-action/card/Party
checks pass. The frontend now draws the returned route, firing line, contact markers and cover
highlights for the selected action; the former browser-calculated estimates are removed. All 13
selected JavaScript scenarios pass, including a late reply for a different actor and an unaffordable
action retaining its current route estimate. Root corrected the simulated browser setup needed by
that completion path.

TD now declares movement AP previews and muzzle-origin firing previews for single, burst and full
auto. All generated runtime content outside `player_controls` is identical before and after this
integration. Ten TD runtime checks with nine subtests pass, including estimated AP matching committed

been generated, and installed preview acceptance remains outstanding.

PES-02 now resolves complete fixed Player Characters and required Companions from the exact selected
Setting's packaged Character Files. Their data must match the Ruleset's participant schema; authored
profiles and mechanical state reach the campaign unchanged. Required Companions are selected
separately from the Plan's additional-companion limits. Incomplete or incompatible files remain
unavailable. Seven campaign-selection and handoff checks pass, including a fixed PC plus required
and custom Companions, stale library content, repeat Start, archive/restore, and GM retrieval after

Ruleset publication now refines its independent Builder title while preserving manual renames.
Default Interview titles follow their accepted campaign names and retain those names when archived;
manual names take precedence even when they match the original default wording. Four Interview
lifecycle/title checks pass, including restart. Sixteen adjacent Builder-library and reusable
participant checks pass. These are source checks; installed acceptance remains outstanding.

Setting publication must follow the project owner's corrected inheritance model in PES-02. A Setting contains its
Ruleset snapshot; a Plan contains its Setting and inherited Ruleset snapshots. Settings and Plans
may customize their copies without changing their sources. Explicit Builder requests may bring
source updates into a snapshot while preserving customizations and asking about conflicts. The
format-4 package and publication changes described above implement this correction. Builder
publishes complete Settings and portable Plans while retaining exact saved sources. PES-08 Ruleset
testing remains deferred, and PES-01 installed journeys remain last.

PES-06 now has a saved layout module (panel locations, groups, sizes and views), connected to the
existing renderers. Campaign overrides take precedence over explicit user Ruleset preferences and
Ruleset order/group/default-placement declarations. Menus and HTML drag/drop use the same movement
operations; extra windows can contain split rows/columns and tabs. Missing current panels retain
their saved assignments, and newly introduced panels start in the sidebar. Map cameras are saved
per campaign/map scene; outgoing selection and confirmation state is cleared on navigation.

Root reviewed and integrated Sol's isolated layout and native-window drafts, correcting null reply,
initial-registration, size-restoration and preference-migration issues. Extra windows receive safe
projections and forward requests through main's existing owner bridge, with current campaign/session
and assigned-panel checks. Main/session closure preserves arrangements; manual extra-window closure
redocks its panels. Party actor selection updates the Map without moving its camera or acting.
Focused source checks cover two-window restoration, shared selection, layout resets, obsolete
requests and monitor fallback. Rust compilation and three focused native tests pass. The current

### Completed prompt/MCP correction record (historical)

#### Prompt, campaign-context, and ruleset-driven MCP correction checklist

This checklist is the durable implementation record for the project owner's 2026-08-17 prompt audit. A checked
item means the described behavior is implemented and proportionately verified; investigation,
partial plumbing, or a passing substitute test is not completion. Update `[ ]` to `[x]` only when
the item's completion evidence exists. The goal is to finish every item in this checklist.

- [x] **1. Record the controlling Ruleset -> engine -> MCP -> GM contract in the active product and
  architecture authorities.**

  **the project owner's intent:** A Ruleset is not merely a collection of callable mechanics. It defines what the
  game engine can do and the state/phase conditions under which each operation is available. The
  engine evaluates those Ruleset declarations against authoritative campaign state every GM turn.
  MCP exposes the resulting currently available operations to the GM. The GM decides which exposed
  operation to call from the Campaign Plan, current scene, and player intent. The provider adapter,
  system prompt, and model must not independently override, recreate, or guess this availability
  policy. The core must remain Ruleset-neutral: SRD-specific availability belongs to the SRD
  package, while another Ruleset can define completely different phases and operations.

  **Implementation required:** Reconcile the active product and architecture contracts with this
  ownership chain and define the terms used by code and tests: Ruleset declaration, authoritative
  state, engine availability evaluation, MCP tool projection, GM choice, GM turn, and mechanic
  commit. Resolve any existing text that implies the provider harness selects mechanics from a
  broad package catalog. Keep this checklist as status only; behavioral authority belongs in those
  contracts.

  **Complete when:** The active contracts state the same ownership model without contradiction,
  their authority mirrors pass, and focused architecture tests or assertions use that model.

- [x] **2. Make mechanic availability fully declarative in each Ruleset package.**

  **the project owner's intent:** The Ruleset itself must say when an operation can be offered. For the SRD,
  starting combat is available while no encounter is active; ordinary out-of-combat D20/DC tests
  are available outside combat when their declared prerequisites permit them; combat-only
  operations become available only during the appropriate encounter, actor turn, and action phase.
  Damage, movement, turn advancement, death checks, recovery, spell operations, and similar tools
  must not all appear merely because an SRD campaign has an open application TURN. Other Rulesets
  must be able to express their own modes without changes to Sylvia's core.

  **Implementation required:** Extend or correctly use the package tool-declaration schema so an
  operation can declare predicates over namespaced authoritative state and, where required, the
  current Ruleset phase/active actor/action budget. Populate the SRD declarations with honest
  availability conditions. Runtime procedure preconditions remain the final execution guard, but
  the same material state must prevent an unavailable operation from being advertised in the first
  place. Do not hard-code SRD tool names or combat rules in the generic engine.

  A Ruleset may additionally bind a non-choice input to its own authoritative `state` or `combat`
  field. Such an input is omitted from MCP and injected at execution, so lifecycle operations do not
  ask the GM to repeat an actor identity the engine already owns. SRD begin/end/advance turn actor
  identities use this neutral mechanism.

  **Complete when:** Package validation rejects invalid availability declarations; representative
  SRD and non-D&D package tests prove different state-dependent tool surfaces; and execution still
  rejects stale or forged calls if state changes after discovery.

- [x] **3. Make the engine calculate the exact current mechanic surface from authoritative state.**

  **the project owner's intent:** “Available this turn” is an engine decision produced from the selected Ruleset
  and current committed state, not lexical similarity to the player's sentence. If combat is
  inactive, combat internals are unavailable even if the player says “attack.” If combat is active,
  the active actor and Ruleset phase determine the legal operations. Every operation the engine
  reports must be callable under the state revision used to report it, subject to arguments and
  ordinary race/staleness rejection.

  **Implementation required:** Add one Ruleset-neutral availability evaluation boundary owned by
  campaign authority. It must evaluate the locked package declarations against the current
  campaign revision, writer, open TURN, Ruleset-owned Campaign State/modes, active actor, and
  declared predicates.
  Its output must retain exact package identity, capability, operation name, description, and input
  schema. Avoid a parallel frontend/provider availability system.

  **Complete when:** Direct engine tests demonstrate the surface changing correctly across
  out-of-combat, combat-started, actor-turn-started, action-spent, turn-ended, and combat-ended
  states, including a neutral non-D&D example.

- [x] **4. Make MCP faithfully expose that engine-calculated surface on every GM inference step.**

  **the project owner's intent:** All model-callable game mechanics are delivered by the engine through MCP. MCP
  must not return the complete installed Ruleset catalog and leave Sylvia or the model to work out
  what is relevant. It returns only the operations the engine says are currently available. Outside
  SRD combat this should be a small surface such as starting an encounter and applicable D20/DC
  resolution—not attack, damage, death-save, recovery, movement, and combat-turn internals. During
  combat it exposes the operations the current combat state permits.

  **Implementation required:** Have MCP `list_tools` query the authoritative availability boundary
  with the campaign and open-TURN identity needed for a current answer. Preserve package identity
  and state/revision validation on `call_tool`. Do not add a second MCP-external package-tool path
  for the GM. Decide and document how a stale advertised surface is rejected cleanly when another
  commit changes its basis revision.

  **Complete when:** Captured MCP `list_tools` responses—not only provider requests—prove the exact
  small surface in each representative state, and every advertised mechanic reaches execution
  through MCP with authority and stale-state checks intact.

- [x] **5. Re-evaluate MCP tools after every committed mechanic before the model continues.**

  **the project owner's intent:** Tool availability can change inside one player-facing exchange. Starting combat
  makes combat-start unavailable and makes the next combat phase available. Beginning an actor's
  turn can expose that actor's actions. Spending an action or ending a turn changes the surface
  again. The GM must never receive the list selected at the beginning of the exchange for all later
  tool rounds.

  **Implementation required:** Treat each post-tool model continuation as a fresh inference step.
  After a committed mechanic, obtain a new authoritative snapshot/revision, call MCP `list_tools`
  again, and rebuild the provider tool definitions. Rejected, awaiting-choice, cancelled, and
  non-mutating results must follow explicit semantics rather than pretending a commit occurred.
  Maintain bounded loop protection without freezing the initial tool list.

  **Complete when:** A single authentic encounter-start sequence captures different MCP/provider
  surfaces before combat, after initiative, after actor-turn start, after action expenditure, and
  after turn/end-combat transitions.

- [x] **6. Remove provider-side keyword tool selection and generic prompt rules that override the
  Ruleset.**

  **the project owner's intent:** Sylvia's provider adapter must not search the player's words for “attack,”
  “cast,” “damage,” “story,” or similar tokens to choose tools. It must not add guessed companion
  tools, silently cap away legal operations, or declare that no mechanic is relevant when the
  engine exposed one. The main system prompt also must not globally forbid an engine-supported
  transition such as starting combat during an opening. The engine/Ruleset owns availability; the
  GM owns the decision to call an available operation.

  **Implementation required:** Delete `_select_model_catalog`-style lexical ranking and its special
  opening suppression. Project the current MCP response directly into provider tool definitions.
  Any unavoidable provider context limit must fail clearly or be solved at the Ruleset operation
  design level; it must not silently substitute heuristic availability. Rewrite mechanics guidance
  so it explains the authority boundary and committed-result discipline without inventing generic
  gameplay restrictions.

  **Complete when:** No live DM path performs keyword-based tool inclusion; prompts contain no
  core-authored SRD/combat phase policy; and adversarial wording tests prove identical engine state
  yields the same MCP surface regardless of player phrasing.

- [x] **7. Replace the fabricated “Begin the story” player turn with a semantic campaign-start
  lifecycle event and support plan-directed opening mechanics.**

  **the project owner's intent:** Beginning a campaign is an application action, not dialogue the player is
  supposed to have said. The GM should receive an application-owned start signal and open the
  accepted campaign immediately. If the Campaign Plan calls for immediate combat, an ambush, an NPC
  action, or another Ruleset-supported opening transition, the GM must be able to use the operation
  currently exposed by MCP. Starting initiative or establishing danger does not itself choose the
  player character's action; the GM must still stop when genuine player agency is required.

  **Implementation required:** Introduce or use a semantic `SESSION_START`/campaign-start input that
  cannot be mistaken for player speech. Remove UI/runtime creation of “Begin the story.” Supply a
  structured opening scene brief derived from the accepted Campaign Plan rather than an exact
  `Open ...` prose-line convention. Let the GM call an exposed start mechanic before or during its
  opening as the plan requires, while retaining explicit player-agency boundaries.

  **Complete when:** Fresh rule-less, peaceful SRD, immediate-combat SRD, and neutral-Ruleset
  campaigns open without fabricated user dialogue; the immediate-combat plan can commit its start
  mechanic; and no test depends on magic opening words.

- [x] **8. Replace the main DM prompt with a compact, stable operating contract.**

  **the project owner's intent:** The GM must understand what Sylvia is, what a Ruleset, Campaign Setting,
  Campaign Plan, character/participant artifact, engine state, MCP mechanic, and campaign history
  each mean, and which source owns which decisions. The prompt should enable confident GM behavior,
  not pile up brittle one-off prohibitions. It must preserve player agency, follow the accepted plan
  without generic substitution, improvise connective material where the plan deliberately leaves
  room, use Ruleset mechanics only through MCP, and narrate committed results honestly. Rule-less
  play must remain genuinely model-led without invented engine enforcement.

  **Implementation required:** Design the prompt in stable sections: identity and role; artifact
  definitions; source/authority precedence; campaign fidelity and permitted improvisation; player
  agency; secrets and reveal discipline; Ruleset/MCP responsibility; state/result fidelity; and
  response/decision-point behavior. Keep volatile scene facts and tool availability out of the
  stable system text. Use provider caching where supported without changing semantics.

  **Accepted revision:** the project owner approved this wording as the current baseline on 2026-08-18. The live
  prompt opens, “You are Sylvia, a Tabletop RPG Game Master, operating within Sylvia GM, a
  specialized environment for AI RPGs.” It next explains Sylvia's purpose in natural language
  before defining how the Setting, Plan,
  participant/character artifacts, history, authoritative state, Ruleset, engine, and per-inference
  MCP surface compose the game. It explicitly introduces the separate application-context message,
  gives a turn-running sequence, distinguishes omniscient GM access from player and character
  knowledge, explains that MCP availability is permission rather than an instruction to call a
  tool, and treats `campaign_start` as an application lifecycle event requiring no fabricated
  “begin the story” confirmation. Exact rule-less and SRD captures were regenerated with this
  revision, and the focused opening, narration, mechanics, rejection, continuation, plan-fidelity,
  and exact-audit checks pass.

  **Complete when:** Rule-less and Ruleset campaign evals demonstrate the intended behavior across
  opening, ordinary narration, mechanical action, rejection, tool continuation, and campaign-plan

- [x] **9. Implement explicit spoiler, secret, and character-knowledge handling.**

  **the project owner's intent:** Campaign Plans and Campaign Settings can contain truths intended only for the GM.
  The player must not be told future reveals, hidden identities, unreached locations, NPC motives,
  or private campaign facts merely because the model received them. “Player-safe narration” and
  “never reveal private reasoning” are not sufficient: secrets are narrative data, not reasoning.
  Reveals should occur only when the plan, world events, investigation, or player choices justify
  them, and NPCs must act from what they know rather than the GM's complete knowledge.

  and uninformed NPC behavior before their reveal conditions, become usable after a committed

## 3. Advertised Alpha journeys

Release work protects these user-visible outcomes:

1. Start a completely general Builder chat from the left sidebar, converse normally, create files,
   Stop, steer, switch models, close, reopen, and continue.
2. Save multiple owner-private provider configurations, discover or enter models, and use an
   already-running local or OpenAI-compatible provider without exposing credentials.
3. Start The Rift Awakening through its supported campaign flow, play with authoritative mechanics,
   save, close, reopen, back up, and recover.
4. Start a no-rules campaign without invented dice, classes, HP, or other unselected game mechanics.
5. Install, launch, restart, and uninstall the Windows application without orphan owner processes or
   renderer-owned authority.
6. Present a stable, understandable interface without severe flicker, missing controls, misleading
   compatibility claims, or internal jargon in ordinary settings.

## 4. Verification relevance fence

For a manual-test correction, verify:

- the focused boundary that caused the defect;
- a representative direct consumer;
- the installed Windows journey affected by the defect; and
- privacy, persistence, restart, or packaging only when the change touches them.

Completed specialist matrices do not automatically direct current testing. FC-14's fixed-rule
requirement applies to the offered 117-spell Lite roster; use shared-owner and representative
composition checks for the current task. Do not improve unrelated subsystems merely because a broad test
mentions them. A repository-wide suite runs only for a genuinely cross-cutting change or at the project owner's
direct request.

## 5. Explicitly post-Alpha

- Trigger Discipline and Siren Protocol; neither is an Alpha blocker;
- the separately preserved Full spell suite and consumables, plus resolvers outside the offered Lite scope;
- bulk custom-ruleset authoring optimization;
- a bundled original ruleset;
- production model benchmarking and ratings;
- additional browser/device-code sign-in beyond the implemented ChatGPT account route;
- Windows 10 and alternate installer qualification;
- paid signing, automatic updates, cloud play, subscriptions, billing, telemetry, marketplace,
  executable third-party plugins, and native macOS/Linux clients;
- import of unreleased predecessor-prototype campaign state.

## 6. Repository and release boundary

- Preserve unrelated work; do not reset, clean, stash, rebase, or overwrite it.
- Exactly one worker writes the live tree.
- Project Desk holds current planning decisions; Git history and this synchronized plan preserve
  repository evidence across environments. Amend existing decisions; do not create a competing plan.
- Staging, commits, and pushes require direct authority in the current human-authored task, as
  specified in `AGENTS.md`. This plan does not grant standing permission for them.
- Public release, credentials, personal campaign data, paid services, signing expenditure,
  destructive operations, and force-pushes require separate authority.
